          Maimonides Medical Center (the “Issuer”) is issuing $100,535,000 aggregate principal amount of its GNMA Collateralized
Taxable Revenue Bonds, Series 2013 (the “Series 2013 Bonds”) pursuant to a Trust Indenture, dated as of March 1, 2013 (the
“Indenture”), by and between the Issuer and U.S. Bank National Association, located in New York, New York, as trustee (the “Trustee”).
The proceeds of the Series 2013 Bonds will be used to (i) acquire the GNMA Securities described below, and (ii) pay certain expenses
incurred in connection with the issuance of the Series 2013 Bonds.
          The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued as fully registered bonds in denominations of $5,000 or any integral multiple thereof.
Interest on the Series 2013 Bonds will be payable on each March 20 and September 20, commencing September 20, 2013 until their
maturity or earlier redemption thereof by check mailed to the registered owners of the Series 2013 Bonds as of the Record Date, as
described herein. Principal payments will be payable on March 20 and September 20 as described herein.
          The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued initially under a Book-Entry Only System, registered in the name of Cede & Co., as
nominee for The Depository Trust Company (“DTC”). Individual purchases of beneficial interests in the Series 2013 Bonds will be made
in book-entry form (without certificates). So long as DTC or its nominee is the registered owner of the Series 2013 Bonds, (i) principal
payments and Redemption Price of, and interest on the Series 2013 Bonds will be made directly to DTC or its nominee, and (ii) all
references in this Official Statement to Bondowners or registered owners shall mean Cede Co., not the beneficial owners of the Series 2013
Bonds. Disbursement of payments to DTC participants is the responsibility of DTC and disbursement of such payments to the beneficial
owners is the responsibility of DTC participants. See “THE SERIES 2013 BONDS - Book-Entry Only System” herein.
          The Series 2013 Bonds have not been registered under the Securities Act of 1933, as amended (the “Securities Act”), or any state
securities laws. The Series 2013 Bonds constitute exempt securities under Section 3(a)(4) of the Securities Act and, as a result, the Series
2013 Bonds and their offering to the public are exempt from registration under the Securities Act of 1933.
          The Issuer will enter into a Financing Agreement (the “Financing Agreement”) with the Trustee and Prudential Huntoon Paige
Associates, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (the “Lender”), pursuant to which the Lender will (i) modify two existing FHA-
insured mortgage loans with the Issuer (the “Mortgage Loans”) and (ii) issue and deliver to the Trustee two fully modified pass-through
mortgage-backed securities in respect of the Mortgage Loans (the “GNMA Securities”) which are guaranteed as to timely payment of
principal and interest by the Government National Mortgage Association (“GNMA”). See “SECURITY AND SOURCES OF PAYMENT
FOR THE SERIES 2013 BONDS” herein.
          Timely payment of principal of, and interest on, the GNMA Securities is guaranteed by GNMA pursuant to Section 306(g) of Title
III of the National Housing Act. Section 306(g) provides that “The full faith and credit of the United States is pledged to the payment of
all amounts which may be required to be paid under any guaranty under this subsection” and an opinion, dated December 12, 1969, of an
Assistant Attorney General of the United States, recites that such guaranties “constitute general obligations of the United States backed by
its full faith and credit”.
          The Series 2013 Bonds are payable solely from the amounts available under the Indenture, primarily constituting moneys
derived from the GNMA Securities and certain funds held by the Trustee, including the investment income thereon. The Series
2013 Bonds do not constitute a debt of, or a pledge of the faith and credit of, the United States of America, the Department of
Housing and Urban Development (“HUD”), GNMA, the Federal Housing Administration (“FHA”) or any other federal
governmental agency.
          The Series 2013 Bonds are subject to redemption prior to maturity as more fully described in this Official Statement and in such
event, investors who purchase Series 2013 Bonds at a price in excess of par risk the loss of any premium paid. See “THE SERIES 2013
BONDS – Redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds – Early Redemption and Loss of Premium” herein.

                                    OFFICIAL STATEMENT

                                              relating to

                          $100,535,000
                  MAIMONIDES MEDICAL CENTER
          GNMA COLLATERALIZED TAXABLE REVENUE BONDS,
                          SERIES 2013

                                  INTRODUCTORY STATEMENT

Purpose of the Official Statement

        The purpose of this Official Statement, including the cover page and appendices hereto, is to set
forth certain information concerning Maimonides Medical Center (the “Issuer” or the “Medical Center”)
and its $100,535,000 GNMA Collateralized Taxable Revenue Bonds, Series 2013 (the “Series 2013
Bonds”).

       The following is a brief description of certain information concerning the Series 2013 Bonds, the
GNMA Securities, the Issuer and Prudential Huntoon Paige Associates, LLC, a Delaware limited liability
company (the “Lender”). A more complete description of such information and additional information
that may affect decisions to invest in the Series 2013 Bonds are contained throughout this Official
Statement, which should be read in its entirety.

Authorization of Issuance

        The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued pursuant to the Trust Indenture dated as of March 1, 2013
(the “Indenture”), by and between the Issuer and U.S. Bank National Association, located in New York,
New York, as trustee (the “Trustee”). All references to funds and accounts in this Official Statement are
to those funds and accounts authorized to be created pursuant to the Indenture. See “THE SERIES 2013
BONDS”.

Description of Plan of Refinancing

         The Issuer will enter into a Financing Agreement (the “Financing Agreement”) with the Trustee
and the Lender, pursuant to which the Lender will modify two existing mortgage loans with the Issuer
that are insured by the Federal Housing Administration (“FHA”), an organizational unit within the United
States Department of Housing and Urban Development (collectively the “Mortgage Loans”) pursuant to
Section 242 and Section 241, respectively, of the National Housing Act of 1934, as amended (the
“National Housing Act”). The Mortgage Loans are evidenced by the Issuer’s mortgage notes
(collectively, as amended, the “Mortgage Notes”), and secured by FHA-insured mortgages on certain real
property owned by the Issuer. The FHA-insured mortgages made with respect to the Mortgage Loans
(collectively, as amended, the “Mortgages”) currently secure the Mortgage Notes which evidence two
loans made with the proceeds of the Dormitory Authority of the State of New York (“DASNY”)
Maimonides Medical Center FHA-Insured Mortgage Hospital Revenue Bonds, Series 1996 (the “Series
1996 Bonds”) and the Maimonides Medical Center FHA-Insured Mortgage Hospital Revenue Bonds,
Series 2004 (the “Series 2004 Bonds”). The Mortgages and the Mortgage Notes will be assigned by
DASNY to the Lender upon the defeasance of the Series 1996 Bonds and the Series 2004 Bonds on the
date of delivery of the Series 2013 Bonds. Following the assignment of the Mortgages and the Mortgage
Notes to the Lender, the Lender will issue, sell and deliver to the Trustee two fully modified pass-through
mortgage-backed securities in the form of Project Loan Certificates (the “PLCs” or the “GNMA
Securities”) in respect of the Mortgage Loans. The GNMA Securities are guaranteed as to timely
payment of principal and interest by the Government National Mortgage Association (“GNMA”) and
such guaranty is further backed by the full faith and credit of the United States of America. Substantially
all of the proceeds of the Series 2013 Bonds (including those derived from original issue premium), will
be used to purchase the GNMA Securities from the Lender. See “ESTIMATED SOURCES AND USES
OF FUNDS” herein. As a result, in the event the GNMA Securities are not delivered by April 4, 2013
(unless such date shall be extended pursuant to the terms of the Indenture), the Series 2013 Bonds shall be
subject to a Special Mandatory Redemption (described herein), and investors will lose any original issue
premium paid in connection with their purchase of the Series 2013 Bonds.

         The GNMA Securities acquired by the Trustee, on behalf of the Issuer, will be deposited by the
Trustee into the Bond Fund and pledged as security for the payment of principal of and interest on the
Series 2013 Bonds. The GNMA Securities will be dated and bear interest from the first day of the month
in which they are issued. Interest on the GNMA Securities for any given month is payable on the
fifteenth day of the following month commencing and is payable at the Pass-Through Rate (which is
equal to the interest rate on the Mortgage Notes less the GNMA guaranty fee and the servicing fee paid to
the Lender).

        The principal of, and redemption premium, if any, and interest on the Series 2013 Bonds are
primarily payable from the payments on the PLCs. In addition to the pledge of the PLCs, the Issuer will
pledge and grant a security interest to the Trustee in all moneys, securities and instruments held from time
to time under the Indenture. The Series 2013 Bonds and the security therefor are more fully described
below under “THE SERIES 2013 BONDS”.

        The Series 2013 Bonds are payable solely from the amounts available under the Indenture,
primarily constituting moneys derived from the GNMA Securities and certain funds held by the
Trustee, including the investment income thereon. The Series 2013 Bonds do not constitute an
obligation or indebtedness of, and the payment of the Series 2013 Bonds is not insured or
guaranteed by, the United States of America or any agency or instrumentality thereof, including
GNMA, HUD or FHA.

The Series 2013 Bonds

         The Series 2013 Bonds will be dated their date of delivery and will bear interest from such date,
payable on September 20, 2013 and on each March 20 and September 20 thereafter until their maturity or
earlier redemption thereof, at the rates set forth on the cover page of this Official Statement. See “THE
SERIES 2013 BONDS”.

The Issuer

       The Issuer is a not-for-profit corporation which is exempt from federal income tax as an
organization described in Section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue of Code of 1986, as amended (the
“Code”). See “APPENDIX A – MAIMONIDES MEDICAL CENTER”.

                                       PLAN OF REFINANCING

Application of Series 2013 Bond Proceeds and Other Moneys

        Substantially all of the proceeds of the Series 2013 Bonds (other than those derived from original
issue premium) will be deposited in the Acquisition Fund. In the event that the PLCs are not delivered by
April 4, 2013 (the “GNMA Securities Delivery Date”) (unless such date shall be extended pursuant to the


                                                     2
terms of the Indenture), amounts deposited into the Acquisition Fund shall be transferred to the Bond
Fund for the redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds on a date not later than April 19, 2013. See “THE
SERIES 2013 BONDS - Redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds – Special Mandatory Redemption”.

        The funds deposited in the Acquisition Fund will be invested in Qualified Investments. It is
currently expected that funds deposited in the Acquisition Fund will be applied to the purchase of the
PLCs from the Lender on or before the GNMA Securities Delivery Date. A portion of the amounts on
deposit in the Bond Fund will be used to pay accrued interest on the PLCs upon purchase thereof. In the
event the GNMA Securities become subject to a Special Mandatory Redemption (described herein) are
not delivered by April 4, 2013 (unless such date shall be extended pursuant to the terms of the Indenture),
and the Series 2013 Bonds shall be redeemed on a date not later than April 19, 2013, investors will lose
any original issue premium paid in connection with their purchase of the Series 2013 Bonds

        The New York State Department of Health approved the plan of refinancing on October 25, 2012.

Payment of the Series 2013 Bonds

       The GNMA Securities will bear interest at the Pass-Through Rate, and the Lender will pass
through principal payments on the Mortgage Notes as principal payments on the GNMA Securities. See
“THE SERIES 2013 BONDS - Redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds”.

        The Indenture provides that the Trustee shall apply amounts on deposit in the Bond Fund on each
Interest Payment Date (or any other date on which Series 2013 Bonds are to be redeemed) to pay the
principal of (whether at maturity or by mandatory redemption), premium, if any, and interest on the Series
2013 Bonds that are payable in accordance therewith.

                                       THE SERIES 2013 BONDS

Description of the Series 2013 Bonds

         The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued as fully registered bonds in the aggregate principal amount
set forth on the cover page hereof. The Series 2013 Bonds will be dated their date of delivery and will
bear interest from such date payable on September 20, 2013 and on each March 20 and September 20
thereafter until their maturity or earlier redemption thereof, and will bear interest at the rates and mature
on the dates set forth on the cover page hereof. Interest on the Series 2013 Bonds shall accrue based upon
a 360-day year of twelve 30-day months.

       The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued in denominations of $5,000 or any integral multiple thereof.
The Series 2013 Bonds will be registered in the name of Cede & Co., as nominee of DTC, pursuant to
DTC’s Book-Entry Only System. Purchase of beneficial interests in the Series 2013 Bonds will be made
in book-entry form, without certificates. See “THE SERIES 2013 BONDS - Book-Entry Only System”.

        Principal of and premium, if any, on the Bonds shall be paid only upon presentation and surrender
thereof for cancellation at the Trust Office of the Trustee. Payment of the interest on each Bond shall be
made by check mailed by first class mail to the person entitled thereto at its address as it appears on the
Bond Register. For so long as the Bonds shall not be held in book-entry only form, upon the request of a
registered owner of at least $1,000,000 principal amount of Outstanding Bonds, all payments of interest
on the Bonds owned by such registered owner shall be paid by wire transfer of immediately available
funds to an account designated in writing by such registered owner. CUSIP number identification with
appropriate dollar amounts for each CUSIP number must accompany all payments of principal, interest or
premium, whether by check or by wire transfer.


                                                     3
Security for the Series 2013 Bonds

       Prior to delivery of the GNMA Securities, moneys on deposit in the funds and accounts under the
Indenture will be invested in Qualified Investments.

        Within approximately 10 day following the assignment of the Mortgages and the Mortgage Notes
to the Lender, the Lender will issue, sell and deliver to the Trustee, and the Trustee will purchase with
substantially all of the net proceeds of the Series 2013 Bonds, two GNMA Securities that will secure the
Series 2013 Bonds.

         Upon delivery of the GNMA Securities, the Series 2013 Bonds will be secured by a pledge of (i)
the GNMA Securities and (ii) such other moneys and funds as may be made available to the Trustee
pursuant to the Financing Agreement and the Indenture. If the GNMA Securities are not delivered by the
GNMA Securities Delivery Date (as such date may be extended pursuant to the Indenture), the Series
2013 Bonds shall be called for Special Mandatory Redemption on April 19, 2013 (or such other date
permitted by the Indenture). See “THE SERIES 2013 BONDS - Redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds –
Special Mandatory Redemption”. The Issuer’s monthly payments on the Mortgage Notes (less the
servicing fee and the GNMA guaranty fee), which are secured by the Mortgages will be passed through
monthly by the Lender to the Trustee. The Trustee will hold such monthly payments and apply them to
interest and principal payments due on the Series 2013 Bonds.

        The Series 2013 Bonds are payable from the above-described sources. The guaranty of GNMA
of the payment of principal of and interest on the GNMA Securities relate only to the timely payment of
principal of and interest on the GNMA Securities and does not provide a guaranty of the payment of
principal of and interest on the Series 2013 Bonds; provided, however, that a failure to make a principal
amortization payment on the Series 2013 Bonds when due is an event of default under the Indenture.
Based on the Cash Flow Statement (as defined in the Indenture), it is anticipated that the interest on and
principal of the GNMA Securities and the Qualified Investments as described above will be sufficient to
pay interest on and principal of the Series 2013 Bonds.

        In the event the Issuer defaults under the provisions of the Mortgages and Mortgage Notes, the
Lender may make a claim for FHA mortgage insurance benefits. Mortgage insurance benefits for an
insured loan are payable by FHA in cash or debentures in an aggregate amount approximately equal to the
outstanding principal amount of a Mortgage Note. If FHA elects to make partial payments in cash, the
Lender may elect to pass through the initial payment to the Trustee in prepayment of the GNMA
Securities and to pay the remaining balance of the GNMA Securities by making the regularly scheduled
payments under the original amortization schedule without recasting the mortgage until the final payment
is received from FHA. When the final cash payment is received from FHA for the mortgage insurance
benefits, the Lender will use such cash, together with other funds of the Lender, to redeem the GNMA
Securities at par plus accrued interest. In the alternative, the Lender may elect to use such initial partial
cash payment, together with other funds of the Lender, to redeem the GNMA Securities in full the month
following receipt of such amount. Such moneys, together with any moneys on deposit in any Fund
established under the Indenture, shall be used to redeem the Series 2013 Bonds in accordance with the
terms of the Indenture. If FHA pays a claim in debentures, the Lender must transfer the debentures to
GNMA in return for GNMA reimbursing the Lender for interest on and principal of the Mortgage Note.

        The Series 2013 Bonds are payable solely from the amounts available under the Indenture,
primarily constituting moneys derived from the GNMA Securities and certain funds held by the
Trustee, including the investment income thereon. The Series 2013 Bonds do not constitute an
obligation or indebtedness of, and the payment of the Series 2013 Bonds is not insured or
guaranteed by, the United States of America or any agency or instrumentality thereof, including
GNMA, HUD or FHA.

                                                     4
Redemption of the Series 2013 Bonds

        The Series 2013 Bonds are subject to redemption prior to maturity as follows:

        Optional Redemption. The Series 2013 Bonds are subject to optional redemption prior to
maturity by the Issuer on any date on or after March 20, 2023, in whole or in part, from payments on the
GNMA Securities representing voluntary prepayments on the Mortgage Loans pursuant to the Financing
Agreement, whether from the proceeds of refunding bonds, other funds of the Issuer or otherwise, at a
redemption price of 100% of the principal amount thereof, plus accrued interest to the redemption date.

        Scheduled Mandatory Redemption. The Series 2013 Bonds are subject to scheduled mandatory
redemption on the respective Interest Payment Dates set forth in the table below, at a redemption price
equal to the principal amount thereof plus accrued interest to the redemption date, in the following
principal amounts, subject to pro rata reduction of such scheduled mandatory redemption payments
pursuant to the Indenture as described below under the heading “Selection of Bonds for Redemption” to
the extent that such Bonds are redeemed prior to maturity otherwise than pursuant to such scheduled
mandatory redemption:

       In the event of a partial redemption of Series 2013 Bonds, the Issuer agrees to deliver a revised
mandatory redemption schedule.

       Special Mandatory Redemption. The Series 2013 Bonds are subject to special mandatory
redemption on the earliest practicable date for which notice of redemption can be given by the Trustee
pursuant to the Indenture, at a redemption price equal to the principal amount thereof redeemed plus
accrued interest to the redemption date, but without premium, as follows:

                                                    5
        (a) In whole, on the fifteenth (15th) day after the GNMA Securities Delivery Date (as such date
may be extended pursuant to the provisions the Indenture), if one or both of the GNMA Securities are not
delivered to the Trustee on or before the GNMA Securities Delivery Date (as such date may be extended
pursuant to the provisions the Indenture).

        (b) As a whole or in part (and if in part, in incremental amounts of one dollar notwithstanding
the minimum authorized denomination of $5,000) on the earliest practicable date (but in no event longer
than 15 days from when the Trustee receives the funds) to the extent that the Trustee receives payments
on the GNMA Securities in excess of regularly scheduled payments (except payments representing
optional prepayments on the Mortgage Loans) including, but not limited to, payments representing:

        (i)    casualty insurance proceeds, condemnation awards or other amounts applied to the
               prepayment of the Mortgage Loans following a partial or total destruction or
               condemnation of either of the projects secured by the Mortgages in the event and to the
               extent that such amounts are not applied to the repair or restoration of the relevant
               project in accordance with the FHA Documents;
        (ii)   mortgage insurance proceeds or other amounts received with respect to the Mortgage
               Loans following the occurrence of an event of default under the FHA Documents;
        (iii) a mandatory prepayment of the Mortgage Loans required by the applicable rules,
              regulations, policies and procedures of FHA or GNMA (including the possible exercise
              by HUD of its right to override the prepayment and premium provisions of the
              Mortgage Notes under certain circumstances); or
        (iv) a prepayment of the Mortgage Loans if HUD determines that prepayment will avoid a
             mortgage insurance claim and is therefore in the best interest of the federal government.

        Selection of the Bonds for Redemption. In the event the Series 2013 Bonds are redeemed in part
and not in whole (other than from principal amortization payments resulting in scheduled mandatory
redemption of Series 2013 Bonds), the Series 2013 Bonds to be redeemed shall be selected by lot and the
scheduled mandatory redemption requirements for each maturity shall be adjusted so that the resulting
debt service on the Series 2013 Bonds (including scheduled mandatory redemption payments) during
each six month period commencing on each Interest Payment Date is proportional, as nearly as
practicable, to the payments on the GNMA Securities during each such six month period, provided
however, that any such adjustment to the debt service on the Series 2013 Bonds shall be conditioned upon
written confirmation from the Rating Agencies of the then current respective ratings on the Series 2013
Bonds.

        Except as otherwise described above, any Series 2013 Bonds to be called for redemption shall be
selected by the Trustee by lot, such selection to be made prior to the date on which notice of such
redemption must be given and Series 2013 Bonds shall be redeemed as soon as practicable after an event
causing a redemption shall have occurred.

         Notice of Redemption. Except as provided below, notice of redemption shall be mailed, first-class
postage prepaid, by the Trustee, not less than 10 days nor more than 30 days prior to the redemption date
(except as otherwise described below), to (a) the respective holders of any Series 2013 Bonds designated
for redemption at their addresses appearing on the bond registration books of the Trustee as of the Special
Record Date, and (b) Digital Assurance Certification LLC (“DAC”) as provided at www.dacbond.com.
Each notice of redemption shall state the date of such notice, the Regular Record Date or Special Record
Date, as applicable, the redemption date, the redemption price, the place or places of redemption
(including the name and appropriate address or addresses of the Trustee), the CUSIP number (if any), the
distinctive numbers of the Series 2013 Bonds to be redeemed and, in the case of Series 2013 Bonds to be

                                                     6
redeemed in part only, the respective portions of the principal amount thereof to be redeemed. Each such
notice shall also state that on said date there will become due and payable on each of said Series 2013
Bonds the redemption price thereof or of said specified portion of the principal amount thereof in the case
of a Series 2013 Bond to be redeemed in part only, together with interest accrued thereon to the
redemption date, and that from and after such redemption date interest thereon shall cease to accrue, and
shall require that such Series 2013 Bonds be then surrendered at the address of the Trustee specified in the
redemption notice.

        Notice of redemption of Series 2013 Bonds shall be given by the Trustee for and on behalf of, and
at the expense of, the Issuer. The Trustee shall mail a second notice to Bondholders with respect to any
Series 2013 Bond called for redemption but not tendered for redemption within 60 days of the redemption
date.

        Failure by the Trustee to give notice pursuant to the Indenture to DAC shall not affect the
sufficiency of the proceedings for redemption. Failure by the Trustee to mail notice of redemption
pursuant to the Indenture to any one or more of the respective holders of any Series 2013 Bonds
designated for redemption shall not affect the sufficiency of the proceedings for redemption with respect
to the Bondholder or Bondholders to whom such notice was mailed.

        Notwithstanding the foregoing or any other provision of the Indenture, in the event of a
redemption pursuant to the Indenture, the Trustee shall give notice of redemption of Series 2013 Bonds
not less than 10 days nor more than 15 days prior to such redemption immediately upon receipt of notice
of prepayment of the Mortgage Loans, in each case from the Lender in accordance with the Financing
Agreement. However, such notice of redemption shall not be required if the circumstances do not permit
the Trustee to give such notice in accordance with the preceding sentence, provided that the Trustee shall
give such notice as soon as practicable.

Early Redemption and Loss of Premium

        Purchasers of Series 2013 Bonds who purchase a Series 2013 Bond at a price in excess of its
principal amount or who hold such a Series 2013 Bond trading at a price in excess of par, should note that
the Series 2013 Bonds are subject to certain early redemptions at a redemption price equal only to their
principal amount plus accrued interest to the redemption date without any redemption premium or the
then market value of the Series 2013 Bonds. This could occur, for example, in the event (i) the GNMA
Securities are not delivered as currently contemplated, (ii) the Mortgage Notes are prepaid as a result of a
default, (iii) there is a default under the Mortgages, or (iv) condemnation or insurance payments with
respect to the Projects are received.

No Additional Bonds

        The Indenture does not authorize the issuance of other Series of Bonds.

Book-Entry Only System

        Unless otherwise noted, the description which follows of the procedures and record-keeping with
respect to beneficial ownership interests in the Series 2013 Bonds, payment of interest and other
payments on the Series 2013 Bonds to DTC Participants or Beneficial Owners of the Series 2013 Bonds,
confirmation and transfer of beneficial ownership interests in the Series 2013 Bonds and other bond-
related transactions by and between DTC, the DTC Participants and Beneficial Owners of the Series
2013 Bonds is based solely on information provided on DTC’s website and presumed to be reliable.
Accordingly, the Issuer, the Trustee, and the Underwriter do not and cannot make any representations
concerning these matters.

                                                     7
        The Depository Trust Company (“DTC”), New York, New York, will act as securities depository
for the Series 2013 Bonds. The Series 2013 Bonds will be issued as fully-registered securities in the
name of Cede & Co. (DTC’s partnership nominee) or such other name as may be requested by an
authorized representative of DTC. One fully-registered Series 2013 Bond certificate will be issued for the
Series 2013 Bonds, in the aggregate principal amount of the Series 2013 Bonds, and will be deposited
with DTC.

         DTC is a limited-purpose trust company organized under the New York Banking Law, a “banking
organization” within the meaning of the New York Banking Law, a member of the Federal Reserve
System, a “clearing corporation” within the meaning of the New York Uniform Commercial Code, and a
“clearing agency” registered pursuant to the provisions of Section 17A of the Securities Exchange Act of
1934. DTC holds and provides asset servicing for over 3.5 million issues of U.S. and non-U.S. equity
issues, corporate and municipal debt issues, and money market instruments (from over 100 countries) that
DTC’s participants (“Direct Participants”) deposit with DTC. DTC also facilitates the post-trade
settlement among Direct Participants of sales and other securities transactions in deposited securities,
through electronic computerized book-entry transfers and pledges between Direct Participants’ accounts.
This eliminates the need for physical movement of securities certificates. Direct Participants include both
U.S. and non-U.S. securities brokers and dealers, banks, trust companies, clearing corporations, and
certain other organizations. DTC is a wholly-owned subsidiary of The Depository Trust & Clearing
Corporation (“DTCC”). DTCC is the holding company for DTC, National Securities Clearing
Corporation and Fixed Income Clearing Corporation, all of which are registered clearing agencies.
DTCC is owned by the users of its regulated subsidiaries. Access to the DTC system is also available to
others such as both U.S. and non-U.S. securities brokers and dealers, banks, trust companies, and clearing
corporations that clear through or maintain a custodial relationship with a Direct Participant, either
directly or indirectly (“Indirect Participants”). The DTC Rules applicable to its Participants are on file
with the Securities and Exchange Commission. More information about DTC can be found at
www.dtcc.com and www.dtc.org.

         Purchases of Series 2013 Bonds under the DTC system must be made by or through Direct
Participants, which will receive a credit for the Series 2013 Bonds on DTC’s records. The ownership
interest of each actual purchaser of each Series 2013 Bond (the “Beneficial Owner”) is in turn to be
recorded on the Direct and Indirect Participants’ records. Beneficial Owners will not receive written
confirmation from DTC of their purchase. Beneficial Owners are, however, expected to receive written
confirmations providing details of the transaction, as well as periodic statements of their holdings, from
the Direct or Indirect Participant through which the Beneficial Owner entered into the transaction.
Transfers of ownership interests in the Series 2013 Bonds are to be accomplished by entries made on the
books of the Direct and Indirect Participants acting on behalf of Beneficial Owners. Beneficial Owners
will not receive certificates representing their ownership interests in the Series 2013 Bonds, except in the
event that use of the book-entry system for the Series 2013 Bonds is discontinued.

        To facilitate subsequent transfers, all bonds deposited by Direct Participants with DTC are
registered in the name of DTC’s partnership nominee, Cede & Co., or such other name as may be
registered by an authorized representative of DTC. The deposit of bonds with DTC and their registration
in the name of Cede & Co. or such other DTC nominee effect no change in beneficial ownership. DTC
has no knowledge of the actual Beneficial Owners of bonds; DTC’s records reflect only the identity of the
Direct Participants to whose accounts bonds are credited, which may or may not be the Beneficial
Owners. The Direct Participants and Indirect Participants will remain responsible for keeping account of
their holdings on behalf of their customers.

        Conveyance of notices and other communications by DTC to Direct Participants, by Direct
Participants to Indirect Participants, and by Direct Participants and Indirect Participants to Beneficial
Owners will be governed by arrangements among them, subject to any statutory or regulatory
requirements as may be in effect from time to time. Beneficial Owners of the Series 2013 Bonds may

                                                     8
wish to take certain steps to augment the transmission to them of notices of significant events with respect
to the Series 2013 Bonds, such as redemptions, tenders, defaults, and proposed amendments to the Series
2013 Bond documents. For example, Beneficial Owners of Series 2013 Bonds may wish to ascertain that
the nominee holding the Series 2013 Bonds for their benefit has agreed to obtain and transmit notices to
Beneficial Owners. In the alternative, Beneficial Owners may wish to provide their names and addresses
to the registrar and request that copies of notices be provided directly to them.

        Redemption notices shall be sent to DTC. If less than all of the Series 2013 Bonds within a
maturity are being redeemed, DTC’s practice is to determine by lot the amount of the interest of each
Direct Participant in such maturity to be redeemed.

        Neither DTC nor Cede & Co. (nor any other DTC nominees) will consent or vote with respect to
the Series 2013 Bonds unless authorized by a Direct Participant in accordance with DTC’s MMI
procedures. Under its usual procedures, DTC mails an omnibus proxy (the “Omnibus Proxy”) to the
Trustee as soon as possible after the record date. The Omnibus Proxy assigns Cede & Co.’s consenting or
voting rights to those Direct Participants to whose accounts the Series 2013 Bonds are credited on the
record date (identified in a listing attached to the Omnibus Proxy).

         Principal, redemption premium, if any, and interest payments on the Series 2013 Bonds will be
made to Cede & Co., or such other nominee as may be requested by an authorized representative of DTC.
DTC’s practice is to credit Direct Participants’ accounts upon DTC’s receipt of funds and corresponding
detail information from the Trustee, on payable dates in accordance with their respective holdings shown
on DTC’s records. Payments by Direct and Indirect Participants to Beneficial Owners will be governed
by standing instructions and customary practices, as is the case with securities held for the accounts of
customers in bearer form or registered in “street name”, and will be the responsibility of such Participant
and not of DTC, the Trustee, or the Issuer, subject to any statutory or regulatory requirements as may be
in effect from time to time. Payment of principal, redemption premium, if any, and interest to Cede &
Co., or such other nominee as may be requested by an authorized representative of DTC, is the
responsibility of the Issuer or the Trustee, disbursement of such payments to Direct Participants shall be
the responsibility of DTC, and disbursement of such payments to the Beneficial Owners shall be the
responsibility of Direct and Indirect Participants.

         DTC may discontinue providing its services as securities depository with respect to the Series
2013 Bonds at any time by giving reasonable notice to the Issuer or the Trustee. Under such
circumstances, in the event that a successor securities depository is not obtained, Series 2013 Bond
certificates will be printed and delivered.

        The Issuer may decide to discontinue use of the system of book-entry-only transfers through DTC
(or a successor securities depository). In such event, Series 2013 Bond certificates will be printed and
delivered.

        The information in this section concerning DTC and DTC’s book-entry system has been obtained
from DTC’s website and is presumed to be reliable, but neither the Issuer, the Trustee nor the Underwriter
takes any responsibility for the accuracy thereof or as to the absence of material adverse changes in such
information subsequent to the date hereof.

                                        GNMA SECURITIES
General

        The summary and explanation of the GNMA Mortgage-Backed Securities Program (the “GNMA
Program”) and the other documents referred to herein do not purport to be complete, and reference is
made to the GNMA I Mortgage-Backed Securities Guide (GNMA Handbook 5500.3, as amended,
hereafter, the “GNMA Guide”) and to said documents, copies of which are on file with the Lender, for
full and complete statements of their provisions.

       GNMA is a non-stock corporate instrumentality of the United States of America within the
Department of Housing and Urban Development (“HUD”) with its principal office in Washington, D.C.

        The GNMA Securities will be “fully modified pass-through” mortgage-backed securities in the
form of Project Loan Certificates (the “PLCs” or the “GNMA Securities”) in respect of the Mortgage
Loans that will be issued and serviced by the Lender. By the 15th day of each month commencing with
the 15th day of the month next following the date of issue of the GNMA Securities, the Lender will be
required to pass through to the Trustee, as the holder of the GNMA Securities, the monthly scheduled
installments of principal and interest on the Mortgage Notes (less the GNMA guaranty fee and the
Lender’s servicing fee), whether or not the Lender receives payment on the Mortgage Notes from the
Issuer, plus any unscheduled prepayments of principal of the Mortgage Notes received by the Lender.
GNMA guarantees the timely payment of the principal of and interest on the GNMA Securities.

GNMA Guaranty

        GNMA is authorized by Section 306(g) of Title III of the National Housing Act, to guarantee the
timely payment of the principal of, and interest on, securities which are based on and backed by mortgage


                                                   10
pools consisting of a single mortgage insured by FHA pursuant to Sections 242 and 241 of the National
Housing Act. Section 306(g) of the National Housing Act further provides that “[T]he full faith and
credit of the United States is pledged to the payment of all amounts which may be required to be paid
under any guaranty under this subsection”. An opinion, dated December 9, 1969, of the then Assistant
Attorney General of the United States of America, states that such guaranties under Section 306(g) of
mortgage-backed securities of the type being delivered to the Trustee on behalf of the Issuer are
authorized to be made by GNMA and “would constitute general obligations of the United States backed
by its full faith and credit”.

         Pursuant to such authority, GNMA, upon delivery of the PLCs to the Trustee in accordance with
the related GNMA Guaranty Agreement, will have guaranteed the timely payment of principal of and
interest on such PLCs.

GNMA Borrowing Authority

        In order to meet its obligations under such guaranty, GNMA, in its corporate capacity under
Section 306(d) of Title III of the National Housing Act, may issue its general obligations to the United
States Treasury Department (the “Treasury”) in an amount outstanding at any one time sufficient to
enable GNMA, with no limitations as to amount, to perform its obligations under its guaranty of the
timely payment of the principal of and interest on the GNMA Securities. The Treasury is authorized to
purchase any obligations so issued by GNMA and has indicated in a letter dated February 13, 1970, from
the then Secretary of the Treasury to the then Secretary of HUD that the Treasury will make loans to
GNMA, if needed, to implement the aforementioned guaranty.

         GNMA warrants to the holder of the PLCs, as described in the GNMA Prospectus, that in the
event it is called upon at any time to make good its guaranty of the payment of principal of and interest on
the PLCs, it will, if necessary, in accordance with Section 306(d), apply to the Treasury for a loan or
loans in amounts sufficient to make payments of principal and interest on the PLCs.

Servicing of Mortgage Loans

       The Lender is responsible for servicing and otherwise administering the Mortgage Loans in
accordance with generally accepted practices of the mortgage banking industry and the GNMA Guide.

        The monthly remuneration of the Lender, for its servicing and administrative functions, and the
guaranty fee charged by GNMA, total 0.25% (25 basis points) per annum (payable monthly) of the unpaid
principal amount of the Mortgage Notes outstanding on the last day of the month preceding such date of
calculation. The Mortgage Notes carry an interest rate that is 0.25% per annum greater than the interest
rate on the GNMA Securities because the 0.25% total of the Lender’s servicing fee and the GNMA
guaranty fee are deducted from payments on the Mortgage Notes prior to making payments on the
GNMA Securities. It is not necessary for GNMA to receive its guaranty fee for the guaranty to be
effective for holders of GNMA Securities.

       It is expected that interest and principal payments on the Mortgage Notes will be the source of
moneys for payments on the GNMA Securities. If such payments are less than what is due, the Lender
may advance its own funds to ensure timely payment of scheduled installments of principal and interest
due on the GNMA Securities. GNMA guarantees such timely payment in the event of the failure of the
Lender to pass through such scheduled principal and interest payments when due.

      The Lender is required to advise GNMA in advance of any impending default on scheduled
payments on the GNMA Securities so that GNMA as guarantor will be able to continue such payments as



                                                    11
scheduled on the 15th day of each month. If, however, such payments are not received as scheduled, the
Trustee, on behalf of the Issuer, has recourse directly to GNMA.

          The Guaranty Agreement to be entered into by GNMA and the Lender in connection with the
PLCs (the “GNMA Guaranty Agreement”) will provide that, in the event of a default by the Lender,
including (a) a request to GNMA to make a payment of principal or interest on the PLCs when the Issuer
is not in default under the Mortgage Notes, (b) insolvency of the Lender, or (c) default by the Lender
under any other guaranty agreement with GNMA, GNMA will have the right, by letter to the Lender, to
effect and complete the extinguishment of the Lender’s interest in the Mortgage Notes, and the Mortgage
Notes will thereupon become the absolute property of GNMA, subject only to the unsatisfied rights of the
holder of the PLCs. In such event, the GNMA Guaranty Agreement will provide that on and after the
time GNMA directs such a letter of extinguishment to the Lender, GNMA will be the successor in all
respects to the Lender in its capacity under the GNMA Guaranty Agreement and the transaction and
arrangements set forth or arranged for therein, and will be subject to all responsibilities, duties and
liabilities (except the Lender’s indemnification of GNMA) theretofore placed on the Lender by the terms
and provisions of the GNMA Guaranty Agreement, provided that, at any time, GNMA may enter into an
agreement with any other eligible issuer of the PLCs under which the latter undertakes and agrees to
assume any part or all of such responsibilities, duties or liabilities theretofore placed on the Lender, and
provided that no such agreement will detract from or diminish the responsibilities, duties or liabilities of
GNMA in its capacity as guarantor of the PLCs, or otherwise adversely affect the rights of the holders
thereof.

        The PLCs do not constitute a liability of, nor evidence any recourse against, the Lender as the
issuer of the PLCs, but recourse thereon is solely against GNMA as provided in the GNMA Guaranty
Agreement and the GNMA Securities. The GNMA Guaranty Agreement provides that the full faith and
credit of the United States of America is pledged to the payment of all amounts required to be paid
pursuant to the GNMA guaranty of such securities.

Payment of Principal and Interest on the GNMA Securities

         Each PLC is a “fully-modified pass through” security which requires monthly payments by the
Lender, as the issuer of the GNMA Securities, to the Trustee, on behalf of the Issuer, of principal and
interest at the rate stated thereon on the aggregate unpaid principal balance, whether or not the Lender
receives payments on the Mortgage Notes underlying such GNMA Securities, plus any prepayments of
principal of the Mortgage Notes received by the Lender.

        Following the issuance of the PLCs and commencement of the payment of principal thereon, the
PLCs will be payable in monthly installments of not less than the interest due on the PLCs the rate
specified thereon, together with any scheduled installments of principal, whether or not collected from the
Issuer and any prepayments or early recovery of principal and premium, if any. Each installment on the
GNMA Securities shall be applied first to interest and then in reduction of the principal balance of the
GNMA Securities then outstanding. Interest shall be paid at the specified rate on the unpaid portion of
the principal. The amount of principal due on the GNMA Securities shall be in an amount equal to the
scheduled principal amortization currently due on the Mortgage Note. Final payment shall be made to the
Trustee upon surrender of the outstanding GNMA Securities.

Liability of the Lender

       The GNMA Securities will not constitute a liability of nor evidence any recourse against the
Lender. Recourse may be had by the Trustee only to GNMA in the event of any failure of timely
payment as provided for in the GNMA Guaranty Agreement appended to the GNMA Securities.



                                                    12
                       CERTAIN PROVISIONS OF THE FHA DOCUMENTS

The Mortgage Notes

         Upon compliance with the terms and conditions of the Lender Commitment, and pursuant to the
terms and conditions specified in the Financing Agreement, the Lender agrees to make a loan to the Issuer
in the aggregate amount of $100,533,682.88 at an interest rate of 3.595% per annum, to be evidenced by
the Mortgage Notes, which are currently and will continue to be insured by FHA. The Mortgage Loans
(i) are insured by FHA pursuant to and in accordance with the provisions of Section 242 and Section 241,
respectively, of the National Housing Act and applicable regulations thereunder, as evidenced by the
endorsement by FHA of the Mortgage Notes; (ii) will have an aggregate outstanding principal balance as
of March 1, 2013 of $100,533,682.88; (iii) bear interest at an interest rate of 3.595% per annum; (iv) as to
the Section 242 Mortgage Loan (defined herein), has a term of 115 months and matures on October 1,
2022 following the modification thereto pursuant to the Second Allonge to Mortgage Note, dated March
21, 2013, and, as to the Section 241 Mortgage Loan (defined herein), has a term of 228 months and
matures on March 1, 2032 following the modification thereto pursuant to the Allonge to Mortgage Note,
dated March 21, 2013; (v) is payable in equal monthly installments of principal and interest following
Commencement of Amortization (as defined in the Indenture); (vi) is secured on a nonrecourse basis
pursuant to the FHA Documents; and (vii) is not subject to prepayment prior to February 28, 2023;
provided, however, that (A) the Mortgage Loans are subject to mandatory prepayment in whole or in part
at any time from the proceeds of any casualty insurance, condemnation award or other amounts received
following a partial or total destruction or condemnation of the Project, in the event and to the extent that
such casualty proceeds, condemnation awards or other amounts are not applied to the repair or restoration
of the Projects in accordance with the FHA Documents; (B) the Mortgage Loans are subject to
prepayment from the proceeds of mortgage insurance or other amounts received with respect to the
Mortgage Loans following the acceleration thereof upon the occurrence of an event of default under the
Mortgages; (C) the Mortgage Loans are subject to prepayment to the extent, if any, required by applicable
rules, regulations, policies and procedures of FHA and GNMA (including the possible exercise by HUD
of its right to override the prepayment lockout and premium provision of the Mortgage Loans under
certain circumstances following an event of default thereunder); and (D) the Mortgage Loans are subject
to optional prepayment on the last day of the month on or after February 28, 2023 at a prepayment price
of 100%, together with accrued interest to the end of the month of prepayment.

       If the Mortgage Loans are prepaid subsequent to the date referenced in clause (vii) above or are
prepaid in accordance with clauses (A) though (D) above, the Lender will give written notice of such
prepayment to the Trustee no later than the fifth day of the month in which the resulting prepayment of
the GNMA Security is to occur.

       In the event of an optional or mandatory prepayment of the Mortgage Loans, in part,
pursuant to the Mortgage Notes, the Issuer unconditionally covenants in the Financing Agreement
to exercise its option to proportionally reamortize the outstanding principal balances of the
Mortgage Notes in accordance with the terms of the Mortgage Notes.

The Mortgages

        Upon the defeasance of the Series 1996 Bonds and the Series 2004 Bonds, the Mortgages will be
modified and assigned to the Lender, as FHA mortgagee, and modified to reflect the terms of the loan to
be made by the Lender. Each Mortgage grants a lien on the properties identified therein together with all
buildings, improvements and fixtures thereon, rents, issues and profits thereof, and all building materials,
equipment, furnishings and other property incident to use and occupancy thereof. Until the final payment
of each Mortgage Note, the Issuer agrees not to sell, encumber or alienate the property subject to each
such Mortgage in any way without the consent of the Lender and FHA, or as otherwise permitted in each

                                                    13
Mortgage or in the related Regulatory Agreement (as defined herein). The Issuer also covenants that it
will not voluntarily create or permit to be created any other lien or liens against the Property subject to the
Mortgages, except for permitted encumbrances.

        The Issuer agrees that, in addition to payments for debt service due on the Mortgage Notes, it will
pay monthly amounts to provide for the payment when due of premiums on the FHA mortgage insurance,
casualty insurance, water rates, and taxes and assessments. In the event the Issuer fails to pay any of the
sums required to be paid under the Mortgages, the Lender, at its option, may pay such amounts. The
Mortgages provide that all sums paid by the Lender may be added to the principal amount of the
Mortgage Notes, bear interest at the rate set forth in the Mortgage Notes and will be due and payable on
written demand. The Issuer covenants that it shall keep the projects secured by the Mortgages in good
repair, and may not make structural alterations without the consents of the Lender and FHA, which
consent shall not be unreasonably withheld, delayed or conditioned.

        Each Mortgage incorporates by reference a regulatory agreement between the Issuer and FHA
(the “Regulatory Agreement”; and collectively with the Mortgage Note, Mortgage and all other
documents required in connection with the modification of a Mortgage Loan by FHA for FHA mortgage
insurance, the “FHA Documents”). The Regulatory Agreement sets forth certain of the Issuer’s
obligations in connection with the management and operation of the project financed with the related
Mortgage Loan (the “Project”). Among its provisions it restricts the use of the Project and the revenues
which it generates and prohibits or restricts transferring, encumbering or altering the Project.

         Under the Mortgages and the applicable FHA regulations, the following are events of default on
the FHA Documents: (i) any default in any payment on the Mortgage Notes or under the Mortgages or
(ii) any failure in the observance or performance of any other covenant in the Mortgages, if the Lender,
because of such failure, has accelerated the debt. If such defaults as defined in (i) and (ii) above continue
uncured after a grace period of 30 days, the Lender shall be entitled to receive mortgage insurance
benefits, provided that within 30 days after the end of the grace period it notifies FHA of the default, and
within 45 days, or any permitted additional time, after the end of the grace period it files with FHA a
notice of its intention to file an insurance claim and its election either to assign the Mortgage Notes and
the Mortgages to FHA or to acquire and convey title to the Projects to FHA. A default solely under the
Regulatory Agreement will not constitute a default under the Mortgages unless declared by the Lender at
the request of FHA. An event of default under the Mortgages is not an Event of Default under the
Indenture and will not automatically result in an acceleration of the Series 2013 Bonds. Under GNMA
regulations, the Lender has broad latitude to enter into workout arrangements and to negotiate
modifications of the Mortgages subject to the approval of FHA and GNMA. So long as the Lender
continues to make regularly scheduled payments of principal and interest on the GNMA Securities no
default will exist as a result of any such mortgage modifications.

FHA and GNMA Documents and Regulations Control

        To the extent that any provision in the Financing Agreement or the Indenture shall be inconsistent
or in conflict with the provisions of the National Housing Act, FHA regulations, related FHA
administrative requirements, the GNMA regulations, the GNMA I Mortgage-Backed Securities Guide,
the FHA Documents, the GNMA Securities or the GNMA Guaranty Agreement, as the case may be, the
provisions of the National Housing Act, FHA regulations, related FHA administrative requirements, the
GNMA regulations, the GNMA I Mortgage-Backed Securities Guide, the FHA Documents, the GNMA
Securities or the GNMA Guaranty Agreement, as the case may be, shall govern. No provision of the
Financing Agreement or the Indenture or of any instruments referred to in the Financing Agreement or the
Indenture shall alter the non-recourse nature of the Lender’s obligations under the GNMA Securities.

departments. Specifically, this modernization resulted in an enlargement of both ambulatory and
inpatient surgery facilities, new and improved state-of-the-art medical technologies, and the
reorganization and enlargement of the Issuer’s Cardiac Institute, the Infants and Children’s Hospital, and
the Emergency Department. The lien of the Section 241 Mortgage is subordinate to the lien of the
Section 242 Mortgage. As of March 1, 2013, the Section 241 Mortgage Loan will be outstanding in the
amount of $90,746,371.78.

         The Section 242 Mortgage Loan and the Section 241 Mortgage Loan are collectively referred to

Mortgage Note are collectively referred to in this Official Statement as the “Mortgage Notes”.

                RISK FACTORS AND REGULATORY PROVISIONS
       WHICH MAY AFFECT THE MEDICAL CENTER OR THE SERIES 2013 BONDS

        The following discussion of risks to holders of the Series 2013 Bonds, in addition to other risks
described throughout this Official Statement, is not intended to be exhaustive, but rather to summarize
certain matters which could affect payment of the Series 2013 Bonds or the financial condition of the
Medical Center.

        The revenue and expenses of the Medical Center are affected by the changing health care
environment. These changes are a result of efforts by the federal and state governments, managed care
organizations, private insurance companies and business coalitions to reduce and/or contain health care
costs including, but not limited to, the costs of inpatient and outpatient care, physician fees, capital
expenditures and the costs of graduate medical education. In addition to matters discussed elsewhere
herein, the following factors may have a material effect on the operations of the Medical Center to an
extent that cannot be determined at this time.

                                                                   16
General
        The Series 2013 Bonds are payable solely from amounts available under the Indenture, including
moneys derived from the GNMA Securities, and the funds and accounts held by the Trustee pursuant to
the Indenture and certain investment income thereon. No representation or assurance can be made that
revenues will be realized from the Medical Center in amounts sufficient to provide funds for payment of
principal of and interest on the Mortgage Notes when due and to make other payments necessary to meet
the obligations of the Medical Center. Further, there is no assurance that the revenues of the Medical
Center can be increased sufficiently to match increased costs that may be incurred.

        There are certain risks which might prevent the Medical Center from obtaining sufficient
revenues to meet all of its obligations, including its obligations under the Mortgage Notes and the
Financing Agreement. Future revenue and expenses of the Medical Center are subject to conditions
which may change to an extent that cannot be determined at this time. Some of the possible changes in
future conditions and other risks are discussed below.

        The receipt of future revenues by the Medical Center is subject to, among other factors, federal
and state regulations and policies affecting the health care industry; and the policies and practices of
managed care providers, private insurers and other third-party payors, and private purchasers of health
care services, all of which may affect rates, costs, third-party payments, and governmental regulations and
policies concerning payment. The effect on the Medical Center of future changes in federal, state and
private policies cannot be determined at this time. Loss of established managed care contracts by the
Medical Center could also adversely affect the future revenues of the Medical Center.

        Future revenues and expenses of the Medical Center and the resulting ability of the Medical
Center to make payments under the Mortgage Notes may be affected by events and economic conditions,
which may include an inability to control expenses in periods of inflation, as well as other conditions such
as demand for health care services; the capability of the management of the Medical Center; the receipt of
grants and contributions; referring physicians’ and self-referred patients’ confidence in the Medical
Center; and increased use of discounted payment schedules through contracts with health maintenance
organizations (“HMOs”), preferred provider organizations (“PPOs”) and other payors. Other factors
which may affect revenues and expenses include the ability of the Medical Center to provide services
required by patients; the relationship of the Medical Center with physicians; the success of the Medical
Center’s strategic plans; the degree of cooperation among and competition with other hospitals in the
Medical Center’s area; changes in levels of private philanthropy; malpractice claims and other litigation;
economic and demographic developments in the United States and in the service areas in which facilities
of the Medical Center are located; changes in interest rates that affect the investment results; and changes
in rates, costs, third-party payments (including, without limitation, Medicare and Medicaid program
reimbursement) and governmental regulations concerning payment. All of the above-referenced factors
could affect the Medical Center’s ability to make payments requirements pursuant to the Indenture. See
“APPENDIX A – MAIMONIDES MEDICAL CENTER” herein.

Adequacy of Revenues; Early Redemption and Loss of Premium
        While the Medical Center is obligated to the Lender under the Financing Agreement to satisfy any
deficiency with respect to the payment of the principal of and interest on the Mortgage Notes, the primary
security for the Series 2013 Bonds are the GNMA Securities and moneys on deposit in various funds and
accounts established pursuant to the Indenture. A default by the Medical Center on the Mortgage Loans
that remains uncured will result in a prepayment of the GNMA Securities and, consequently, a
redemption of all or a portion of the Series 2013 Bonds.

       In addition, purchasers of Series 2013 Bonds who purchase a Series 2013 Bond at a price in
excess of its principal amount or who hold such a Series 2013 Bond trading at a price in excess of par,

                                                    17
should note that the Series 2013 Bonds are subject to certain early redemptions at a redemption price
equal only to their principal amount plus accrued interest to the redemption date without any redemption
premium or the then market value of the Series 2013 Bonds. This could occur, for example, in the event
(i) the GNMA Securities are not delivered as currently contemplated, (ii) the Mortgage Notes are prepaid
as a result of a default, (iii) there is a default under the Mortgages, or (iv) condemnation or insurance
payments with respect to the Projects are received. See “THE SERIES 2013 BONDS - Redemption of
the Series 2013 Bonds”.

Health Care Reform

        In March 2010, President Obama signed into law comprehensive health reform through the
Patient Protection and Affordable Care Act (Pub. L. 111-148) and the Health Care and Education
Reconciliation Act of 2010 (Pub. L. 111-152), together known as the Affordable Care Act (“ACA”).
ACA was intended by its supporters to be transformative and includes numerous provisions affecting the
delivery of health care services, the financing of health care costs, reimbursement of health care
providers, and the legal obligations of health insurers, providers, employers and consumers.

        The U.S. Supreme Court decided the constitutionality of certain ACA provisions in National
Federation of Independent Business v. Sebelius: (i) the “individual mandate” that requires individuals to
purchase health insurance starting in 2014 or be penalized, and (ii) the expansion of the Medicaid
program. On June 28, 2012, the Supreme Court upheld the individual mandate but held that states have
the option to reject the Medicaid expansion provisions without the risk of losing their federal Medicaid
funding.

        Controversy over ACA continues even after the Supreme Court’s decision. While the reelection
of President Obama and the continued Democratic control in the U.S. Senate will be a major force in
defending ACA, the Supreme Court’s consideration in its 2012-13 term of a challenge to ACA’s
employer insurance requirements may negatively affect ACA’s future. Additionally, the U.S. House of
Representatives has voted several times to repeal ACA, in whole or in part, and to cut funding for various
ACA provisions; however, none of these efforts have passed, and after the recent elections are more
unlikely to pass, in the U.S. Senate. Regardless, even if the ACA’s future is more secure, it is impossible
to predict its potential impact on the health care industry.

         As ACA and its implementing regulations will be phased in over several years on both a federal
and a state level, the full consequences of ACA on the health care industry have not been immediately
recognized. Therefore, it is difficult to predict whether the net impact of specific provisions of ACA will
be positive or negative on the Medical Center, its revenues and its financial condition. Moreover,
uncertainties regarding the implementation of ACA on a national level and in New York State create
unpredictability for the strategic and business planning efforts of the Medical Center. Some of the
specific provisions of ACA that may affect the Medical Center’s operations, financial performance or
financial condition are described below. This listing is not intended to be, nor should be considered by
Series 2013 Bondholders as comprehensive. ACA is complex, and includes a myriad of new programs,
initiatives, and changes to existing programs, policies, practices and laws. For a comprehensive
understanding, Series 2013 Bondholders should review ACA in its entirety.

        Insurance Changes

         Under ACA, new state-based health insurance exchanges, increased employer insurance coverage
requirements, and Medicaid expansions will likely decrease the number of uninsured patients. This
increase in insured patients could result in lower levels of bad debt and increased utilization or profitable
shifts in utilization patterns for hospitals generally. Nonetheless, it remains unclear how these ACA



                                                     18
insurance changes will impact the Medical Center. For example, the ultimate provider rates under, and
the comprehensiveness of, the New York exchange plan are still uncertain.

        While statements by Governor Cuomo indicate that New York is leaning towards participating in
the Medicaid expansion available under ACA, it is still uncertain whether New York will participate, and
exactly how this Medicaid expansion will impact New York hospitals. In August 2012, the New York
State Department of Health (“DOH”) submitted its Medicaid Redesign Team (“MRT”) Waiver
Amendment to CMS seeking $10 billion in federal funds over a five year period for investment in New
York State’s health care system to be funded from Medicaid redesign savings. If obtained, these funds
will be used to implement MRT and ACA provisions, including the Medicaid expansion. See “State
Budget” herein.

        Payment Rules

        ACA will increasingly link payments to hospitals under public programs to quality and
coordination of care. Accordingly, if the Medical Center does not meet the quality status requirements,
the Medical Center and its revenues could be adversely impacted. Regardless of the hospital’s
performance, beginning in federal fiscal year 2013, Medicare inpatient payments to hospitals, including
Medicare HMO payments, will be reduced by 1% progressing to 2% by federal fiscal year 2017.
Beginning in fiscal year 2013, hospitals will be subject to Medicare payment withholds or bonuses
contingent on satisfaction of specific performance measures related to common and high-cost medical
conditions under a new value-based purchasing program and face payment reductions based on the dollar
value of preventable readmissions for certain medical conditions. Furthermore, ACA allows the Centers
for Medicare & Medicaid Services (“CMS”) to expand the conditions measured for readmission rate
penalties beginning in federal fiscal year 2015.

         In addition, beginning in federal fiscal year 2015, Medicare inpatient payments to hospitals that
are in the top quartile nationally for frequency of certain “hospital-acquired conditions” will be reduced
by 1% for all discharges for the applicable federal fiscal year. As of July 1, 2011, CMS no longer
provides federal funding to states for any amounts expended by providers in treating so-called provider-
preventable conditions. CMS also directed states to submit amendments to their Medicaid state plans to
require payment denials for the cost of treating such conditions, consistent with the prohibition on federal
reimbursement. DOH issued an emergency regulation, effective December 6, 2011, that denied payment
for several “potentially preventable negative outcomes.”

        ACA also contains reductions in Medicare market basket updates and cuts in Medicare and
Medicaid disproportionate share hospital (“DSH”) payments for providing care to low income and
uninsured patients. Generally, Medicare payment rates to hospitals are adjusted annually based on a
“market basket” of estimated cost increases, which have averaged approximately 2-4% annually in recent
years. ACA provides for a general reduction of a specified percentage (up to 0.75% per year) in each
federal fiscal year through 2019. There is also a productivity adjustment based on national economic
productivity statistics which resulted in a 1% reduction in the market basket update for federal fiscal year
2012 and is expected to have a similar result in future years. Separately, beginning in federal fiscal year
2014, Medicaid DSH allotments to each state will also be reduced. See “Medicare and Medicaid
Reimbursement–DSH Payments” herein for background on DSH funding.

         While the House of Representatives has voted to repeal the Medicare Independent Payment
Advisory Board (“IPAB”), ACA directs the IPAB to provide recommendations to Congress on ways to
curtail growth of Medicare expenditures beginning in 2014. If Congress does not adopt alternative
legislation to meet the savings targets, ACA provides for the IPAB’s recommendations to be
automatically implemented. Starting in 2020, hospitals will be subject to the IPAB’s cost reduction
proposals.

                                                    19
        ACA contained significant changes to federal payments to Medicare health management
organizations – Medicare Advantage plans (“MAPs”) – that could have an effect on MAPs’ payments to
hospitals. Federal payments to MAPs are tied to the level of fee-for-service spending in the applicable
county, resulting in a reduction for certain MAPs. These reduced payments could in turn affect the scope
of coverage of these plans or cause plan sponsors to negotiate lower payments to providers. See
“APPENDIX A – MAIMONIDES MEDICAL CENTER–Sources of Patient Service Revenue” herein.

        Fraud and Abuse

        ACA included significant changes to health care fraud and abuse laws that will intensify the risks
and consequences of enforcement actions. These changes include: expansion of the Federal False Claims
Act; lessening of the intent requirements under the federal Anti-Kickback Statute; and new funding and
expanded powers to investigate fraud, including through expansion of the Medicare Recovery Audit
Contractor (“RAC”) program and the requirement that states create a Medicaid RAC program. New
York’s State Plan Amendment for the Medicaid RAC program was approved in March 2011 and
designated HMS as its RAC contractor in April 2011. ACA also creates enhanced penalties for
noncompliance, including increased criminal penalties and expansion of administrative penalties under
Medicare and Medicaid. In addition to these potential noncompliance penalty costs, all hospitals will
eventually be required to establish and maintain compliance and ethics programs that satisfy certain
federal requirements as a condition of enrollment in Medicare, Medicaid and the Children’s Health
Insurance Program. The New York’s Office of the Medicaid Inspector General 2012-13 Work Plan
emphasizes enforcement of ACA requirements, specifically discussing the identification and refunding of
overpayments. See “Federal Fraud and Abuse Laws and Regulations” and “Federal and State False
Claims Acts” in this section and “APPENDIX A – MAIMONIDES MEDICAL CENTER – Material
Pending Audits” herein.

Budget Control Act

         The Budget Control Act of 2011 was enacted August 2, 2011, thereby bringing an end to the debt
ceiling crisis that had threatened to lead the United States into sovereign default on or about August 3,
2011. The law specifies $917 billion of cuts over 10 years in exchange for the initial debt limit increase
of $900 billion, with approximately $21 billion of cuts to be applied in the fiscal year 2012 federal
budget. In order to achieve those budget cuts, and to try to reduce the long-term federal debt, the law
introduces several complex mechanisms. Since Congress failed to produce a deficit reduction bill with at
least $1.2 trillion in cuts over the coming 10 years by December 23, 2011, then any further increase in the
debt ceiling will trigger across-the-board spending cuts equally split between security and non-security
programs. The across-the-board cuts would apply to mandatory and discretionary spending in the years
2013 to 2021 in an amount of $1.2 trillion, and are scheduled to be automatically implemented on January
2, 2013. In September 2012, the White House Office of Management and Budget issued a report
detailing the possible cuts, with Medicare beneficiaries having no changes in their benefits but providers,
including hospitals, having an across-the-board 2% cut to Medicare reimbursements. While the Medical
Center cannot predict whether across-the-board cuts will be triggered, substantial reductions in federal
health care spending through Medicare, Medicaid and other programs seem probable, and any such
reductions are likely to negatively impact the Medical Center and its revenues.

Economic Turmoil
         The current economic turmoil, including the downgrade of the long-term credit rating of the United
States by Standard & Poor’s Ratings Service, has had and will continue to have negative repercussions upon
the United States and global economies. In the last few years, this turmoil has particularly affected the
financial sector, prompting a number of banks and other financial institutions to seek additional capital, to
merge, and, in some cases, to cease operating. These events collectively have led to a scarcity of liquidity


                                                     20
and credit, lack of confidence in the financial sector, volatility in the financial markets, fluctuations in interest
rates, reduced economic activity, increased business failures, and increased consumer and business
bankruptcies.

         Hospitals are required to provide emergency care without regard to a patient’s ability to pay. Poor
economic conditions and increased unemployment can enlarge the population that does not have health care
coverage and thus cannot pay for care out-of-pocket, which in turn can increase the uncompensated care
provided by the Medical Center. Tax-exempt hospitals, in particular, often treat large numbers of indigent
patients who are unable to pay in full, or perhaps at all, for their medical care. In addition, poor economic
conditions and increased unemployment can lead patients to postpone or forego elective procedures, thereby
reducing volume and revenue. See “Not-for-Profit Status – IRS Examination of Compensation Practices and
Community Benefit” and “Internal Revenue Code Limitations – Charity Care” herein.

          If the current economic turmoil continues and the economy further weakens, health care providers
could be materially and adversely impacted in a number of ways, including reduced investment income,
reduced philanthropic donations, reduced access to the credit markets, difficulties in obtaining new liquidity
facilities or extensions of existing liquidity or other short-term facilities, significant draws on internal
liquidity due to difficulties with remarketing existing variable rate bonds and commercial paper, increased
risk of acceleration on variable rate bonds or rollover risk of short-term indebtedness, increase in bad debt
expense and charity care write-offs, and increased borrowing costs, any of which may negatively affect the
operations or financial condition of the Medical Center.

Legislative, Regulatory and Contractual Matters Affecting Revenue
         The health care industry is heavily regulated by the federal and state governments. A substantial
portion of revenue comes from governmental sources. Governmental revenue sources are subject to
legislative and policy changes by the governmental and private agencies that administer Medicare,
Medicaid, other third-party payors and governmental payors, and actions by, among others, The Joint
Commission, CMS and other federal, state and local government agencies. These agencies have broad
discretion to alter or eliminate programs that contribute significantly to revenues of the Medical Center.
In the past, there have been frequent and significant changes in the methods and standards used by
government agencies to reimburse and regulate the operation of hospitals. See “Health Care Reform”
herein for more information on current and proposed future changes in hospital reimbursement. No
assurances can be given that further substantial changes will not occur in the future or that payments
made under such programs will remain at levels comparable to the present levels or be sufficient to cover
all existing costs. While changes are anticipated, the impact of such changes on the Medical Center
cannot be predicted. The Medical Center is exploring the possibility of forming accountable care
organizations (and has formed a health home) and entering into risk based (capitation) agreements, which
would change the mentality of care delivery to one of promoting the wellness of a population of patients,
rather than treating diseases and other conditions that result from poor health maintenance. The
expectation of this evolving philosophy is to ultimately reduce the cost of health care and, therefore,
benefit patients and providers alike.

         The Medical Center has established estimates, based on information presently available, of
amounts due to or from Medicare and non-Medicare payors for adjustments to current and prior years’
payment rates, based on industry-wide and Medical Center-specific data. The current Medicaid,
Medicare and other third-party payor programs are based upon extremely complex laws and regulations
that are subject to interpretation. Medicare cost reports, which serve as the basis for final settlement with
government payors, have been settled through 2008. The Medical Center is not aware of any allegations
